# Week of 2026-11-08 (THEO, home X1-PT36; next reset ~2026-11-15 13:00 UTC)

Format: see README.md. Previous week: 2026-11-01.md (297.7M wallet, 461M trading net). Opened 2026-11-10 at the operator's request; days before that are thinner. Read this first each session, then append.

## Standing rules (operator)

- Use only the Tailwinds MCP tools (`mcp__Tailwinds__stcommand_*`) for every fleet read and write; never the lowercase `stcommand` server (suspended, 503).
- Approvals are the operator's decision. Leave fuel warnings out of updates unless a ship is truly stranded.
- **Buy a ship at the yard nearest where it will trade, not the cheapest** (see `CLAUDE.md`, "Buying a ship"; written 2026-11-10).
- **Trade volume is the growth lever, but only repeatable volume into freshly covered systems during the ~2h premium** — not
  just more units on stale routes.
- Intermediate hull is the **light hauler (~315k)** over 82k shuttles. **Heavy freighter ~2.55M / bulk ~3.29M once cash is around
  10M**, then start selling the small ships.
- **Cash discipline:** buy no hull (light haulers included) until the wallet holds **3M**; keepers wait for **5M**. Escape
  clause: if we are *struggling to get there* (cash flat/falling ~2h, rate under ~80k/h, or idle traders), break the rule and
  buy a hauler to restart growth.
- Opening v3 runs by itself (saved template "Opening: auto at reset", tasks o3-*-1108): THEO-1 tours then trades; 200k keepers on the
  shipyards; 200k contract shuttle; 1M keepers on the top markets; 1M light hauler on the gate crew with gate buying; FAB_MATS ceiling
  2,000, ADVANCED_CIRCUITRY ceiling 4,000. Adjust in real time (operator, 2026-11-08).

- 2026-11-10 13:55 **Operator: release the home system and find out why it was queued ("it shouldn't have been").** Done: `release_system X1-PT36` at about 13:30 (ok, markets D49, no keepers unparked); read back: dispatcher routes 6 to 24, THEO-16 assigned COPPER_ORE contractBuy at PT36-J62 (rejectedCount 23 there, to look at), THEO-1E and THEO-7 working AR37 routes. Cause (read from the code): at 12:57:14 the log shows "THEO-31 jump: X1-PT36: skipped 6 thin market(s)", i.e. a keeper probe jumping through the home hub toward BY84. With `remoteSurveyIntake` on, `FleetManager.judgeAndIntake()` (fleet.ts, called after every fleet jump) judged X1-PT36 like a newly surveyed system; its only guard was "already in the queue"; the home system is not in the queue (it is live from the start), passed the worth test, and `intakeKeeperCandidates` queued it with the one market that was not on the keeper priority list (D49), as scouted. Queued and not live means gated: all its routes were held, route count 43 to 6, no sale from 12:50 to 13:24. Fix written (not yet deployed): pure `systemAlreadyLive()` in keeperBuy.ts (true for the home system, or any system with a keeper already on one of its markets), used as an early return in `judgeAndIntake()` and `intakeKeeperCandidates()`; 3 new tests, tsc clean; CHANGELOG entry added. Until the box deploys main, another keeper jump through the hub could queue it again: watch `ops system_queue` for X1-PT36 "scouted", and the hourly update checks it. Cost of the stall: about 33 minutes with no sales (baseline about 50-60k an hour) plus idle traders. Still open: THEO-16's rejectedCount 23 at PT36 (23 routes rejected for it), and the 13:12 update that fired at 13:21.

  106,145. Cash 173,239 at 21:11 → 523,511 at ~22:00 → 208,523 after the hauler buy. 429s 0, queue 0, API 7–21% of the 105 cap.

  repeats of 2026-11-01 lessons:
  1. **Over-bought keepers.** 39 keepers + 14 parked = 53 intel hulls vs 9 traders. Last week's gate lesson: "one touring
     shuttle instead of early keepers, keepers only on the gate's producers and their inputs." ~1.5M of probes is capital that
     should have been haulers — the post-gate rate is 112k/h, capacity-bound.
  2. **Released X1-DJ26 + X1-FR76 together and bought keepers.** Last week: "staging several systems at once costs about 490k an
     hour in probe jumps; stage one at a time closer to the keeper yards." This week positioningCosts 156,098/24h (4.45%), and
     21 jumps / 156,799c in 80 min.
  3. **Thin-market big buys.** THEO-21 bought 62u MEDICINE for 296,112 (and 63u for 283,059) into a tv-100 market — the buy-side
     of the thin-market lesson. Tied up ~300k per load for a thin margin. Capped to lotSize 20 × maxLots 2.
  4. **Manual cross-system routes cost ~100k in jumps in 80 min** (THEO-44 46k, THEO-1E 27k, THEO-1 14k, THEO-21 13k). Cleared.
- **Action:** cleared THEO-1E and THEO-44 routes; capped THEO-21 lots; `manual routes remaining: []`.
- **Plan to lift the rate:** freeze keeper/probe purchases (33 priority markets uncovered — not the bottleneck); put every spare
  credit into light haulers (~315k, ~40k/h, ~8h payback); keep traders on the high-per-unit systems (PT36 last bucket 558,
  BY84 726, FR76 294).
- **Open:** rate 112k/h vs the 2026-11-01 baseline of 1.4-1.9M/h (scale differs — that fleet had ~350 ships).
- **Finding (00:40, "right number before buying keepers again"):** the control is `keeperAutoApproveMinCash` (currently
  **3,000,000**, max 5,000,000) — below it every keeper probe asks the operator instead of auto-buying. The companion gate is
  `systemReleaseMinCash` (currently **100,000**), which is what let DJ26+FR76 stage/release with almost no float. A fresh
  system costs `freshKeepersPerSystem` 8 × ~30k ≈ **240k** (+jumps). The point of the number is not cash for its own sake: a
  release only pays the ~1.7x premium if enough trade hulls exist to run its routes (this week's systems ran 146-315/unit with
  too few hulls). **Recommendation: `keeperAutoApproveMinCash` 5,000,000 and `systemReleaseMinCash` 1,000,000** — at 5M the
  fleet can hold ~1.5M trader float, fund two fresh systems (~500k) and still buy 6-8 light haulers. Proposed, not yet applied.
- **Action (00:35, operator "Make those changes. We just have to stick to it"):** applied the keeper cash gate —
  `keeperAutoApproveMinCash` **3,000,000 → 5,000,000** and `systemReleaseMinCash` **100,000 → 1,000,000** (verified in
  `get_doctrine`). Effect: no automatic keeper probes below a 5M wallet, and staging a system can never leave the wallet under
  1M. The engine still lists "wanted a release" but has nothing staged and cannot stage below the gate. This is the number for
  the week; keepers wait until the trading fleet is capitalized.
- **Rule (00:45, operator):** no hull purchases — light haulers included — until the wallet holds **3M** (keepers still 5M,
  `keeperAutoApproveMinCash`). Manual `buy_ship` bypasses doctrine, so it is a by-hand discipline rule. **Escape clause
  (operator):** "unless we start struggling to get there" — if cash is flat/falling ~2h, the rate drops under ~80k/h, or
  traders sit idle, break the gate and buy a hauler to restart growth. Mirrored into `CLAUDE.md` ("Buying a ship").
- **Action (02:0x, operator "revert the switch"):** reverted the blunt global route-shape cap — `hauledHops` back to
  8/off and `positioningHops` back to 10. It was a fleet-wide limit that would also have refused 2-gate legs for the
  freighters we plan to buy.
- **Action:** wrote **`docs/slow-hull-local-routes-spec.md`** for the engineer agent — a per-speed route-shape clause
  (`slowHullSpeed` 15, `slowHullHauledHops` 1, `slowHullPositioningHops` 2), applied only to hulls at/below the speed
  threshold, with implementation points in `haulLimit.ts` / `dispatcher.ts` / `fleet.ts`, tests, acceptance criteria and
  a safe rollout (keys ship disabled).- **Action (03:4x, slow-hull clause enable→disable):** after deploying `dc39c5d` (build + `docker restart stcommand` on the box;
  `/version.json` loadedFileMtime 03:23:35Z; three keys present), enabled `slowHullSpeed=15`, `slowHullHauledHops=1`,
  `slowHullPositioningHops=2`. Observed loaded share 0.38 (was 0.35) and no "slow hull" refusals — the current board is all
  local or 1-gate (DJ26→AC33 and AC33→PT36 are 1 hop), so nothing was over the cap. **Operator: "switches off — we don't have
  any hulls over 15 right now so nothing will take long routes."** Disabled all three (values kept). The clause only helps a
  mixed fleet: turn it on when the first speed-36/60 freighter arrives, so it gates the speed-15 hulls and exempts the fast ones.
- **Rule (04:5x, operator):** keeper-probe approvals — **approve probes whose target market is a shipyard; never deny the rest.** Leave non-shipyard keeper cards pending for the operator to decide. (This corrects the earlier auto-deny while cash builds.) Context: 21 known shipyards, 11 covered, 10 not (AC33-A14E staged, AR37-DE3Z/ZA6A parked, BY84-DD3F parked, DJ26-CC4B pending, HA25-D21F/X14C/Z12D staged, VN83-BZ6B/CC8E staged). Shipyard keepers keep yard stock/prices fresh, which the nearest-yard buying rule depends on.

## Targets (operator, 2026-11-11 ~17:00Z) — growth ramp vs last week

Baseline: 10 traders (1 heavy THEO-64), 9 live systems, wallet 1.65M / wallet+holds 2.97M, rate ~312k/h.
Last week at the same point: ~27 live systems, 20-30 traders, 16M+ wallet. The gap is scale (capital + hulls +
live systems), not strategy. Plan: let cash build, then light haulers in batches; fund one staged system's keepers
alongside. Light haulers beat heavies on capacity per credit (80/315k ~0.25 vs 225/2.55M ~0.09).

  loaded share **>= 0.45**.
- **By 2026-11-13 06:00Z:** wallet+holds **>= 10M** (heavy/bulk line); traders **>= 22**; live systems **>= 25**.

3M hold and buy a hauler to restart growth. If ahead, keep reinvesting — do not let cash sit above ~4M unspent.

  (that clause is an AND on top of keepers, and it is off). `queueTours.ts` only sends tour ships. So this week's auto mode waited on keepers while THEO-4B had already priced YK92.

## 2026-11-12 04:15Z — built the tour-priced release clause (operator: "build the doctrine we need to automate this")
- **Answer to "does the code already send a tour ship to the next queued system?":** yes. `freshTours` (value = most ships, 2 here, enabled) runs `queueTours.ts` after each fresh-system check:
  free tour/explorer ships go to staged systems, nearest first, ready systems first, and stay until `releaseMinPricedPct` (80%) of the markets are priced. Its limits here: only 2 tour ships
  (THEO-4A, 4B), so YR4/CU32/F39 log "need a tour: no tour or explorer ship". What it did NOT do was release.
- **Built:** clause `releaseOnTourPct` (50%, default off). A staged system with a tour/explorer ship in it and at least that share priced is ready without 80% of its keepers; keeper-ready systems still go
  first; target, gap, holds, reachability unchanged. `src/engine/systemRelease.ts`, `doctrine.ts`, `fleet.ts` (fills `tourHere`), 5 tests in `tests/systemRelease.test.ts` (29 pass), CHANGELOG entry.
  Pushed to the feature branch and to `main` (f96fc0d); typecheck clean. The 3 failing `doctrine.test.ts` subtests fail identically on the parent commit (catalog counts, tenant default), not caused by this.
- **Not live yet:** the box deploys from `main`, so it needs a deploy/restart; then `set_doctrine releaseOnTourPct` 50 and enable. Until then releases are by hand (YK92 done 03:44).
- **Local test DB:** created role/db `claude_test` on the sandbox Postgres to run the DB tests (sandbox only).

## 2026-11-12 12:45Z — where the gate-open time is recorded
The engine logs a `gate_open` milestone in `action_events` (kind milestone, subject gate_open); the compare endpoint (`src/http/compare.ts`) and ops `analysis` (`summary.gateOpenHours`, `milestones`) read it. This week: home gate open at 2026-11-10 12:15:46Z, 47.2 h into the week (27 ships, 772,397 credits), about 3.4 h before last week's ~hour 50.6 first jump. ops `gate_history` only shows material readings, so it is the wrong tool for the open time. Hourly "Gate pace" line: take gate-open from ops analysis milestones.

I could not prove which applied; the log only prints the count, not the reason per ship. Worth adding a per-ship reason to the note.

## 2026-11-12 19:35Z — the neighbourhood idea, as the operator described it
Operator: a hub ("neighbourhood") is a big system plus the active systems around it; several run at once; ships stay inside their own; the engine then cannot hand out a crazy route across the galaxy. For next week. Written into docs/TODO.md item 9 (feature branch only, not on main). Evidence this week for sizing it: jumps cost 0.39-0.60M of every 2.4-3.0M hour in the 17:00-19:00 hours (55-87 jumps at about 6.9k each, about 15-20% of the hour's profit), each gate hop is followed by about a 9 minute cooldown, and the tour ships needed 1+ hour to walk to staged systems 5-10 hops out. Manual levers that exist today: `set_ship_reach system` (local-only ships; they get what cross-system ships leave) and the doctrine cap on jumps to a route's buy market (default 3, hard ceiling 10). Not done: grouping systems, a home per ship, a dispatcher bonus/penalty, per-neighbourhood tours and yards.

More lanes spread the keeper reads and give spares, but will not lower the box's action calls. Whether keeper rotation returns to about 7 min is the thing to watch at the 02:12 update; if it does not, the budget that throttles keepers (90 calls a minute in keeper_review) is a separate setting to look at.

## 15:30Z: keeper jump errors investigated (user: "dig into that"), plus a database disk-full warning

- **Cause found, harmless to trading:** the failing `keeper:jump` calls are new keeper probes hopping toward staged systems. Log lines like `THEO-395: hop to X1-AZ61 (toward X1-AZ61) failed, will retry: Ship action is still on cooldown for 172 second(s)` (also THEO-38B, 390, 39B, 39C, 39D, 1B0, 4B). In `hopToward()` (`src/engine/fleet.ts`, the catch after `jumpShip`) the cooldown error is logged and returns false, so the next tick tries again at once instead of waiting out the cooldown.
- **Pattern:** the error bursts follow each batch of probe purchases: 10:15 (82 errors in 15 min), 11:15 (93), 12:30-12:59 (84), 14:45-15:15 (168); quiet between. About 85% of keeper jump calls fail in a burst (e.g. 107 of 126 at 14:45). `keeper:ship` refreshes rose from about 3 to 13 a minute with them, so a burst wastes roughly 13 box calls a minute (about 14% of the 105 cap). No trade is blocked.
- **Fix not applied** (read-only investigation, no code change): written up as TODO item 9c (wait out the cooldown with `Pending` and `resumeAt`). The ship behind it was not one ship but every new probe in transit.
- **Separate and more serious: the Postgres disk was full at 15:01-15:02.** Log lines: `could not extend file "base/16385/180555": No space left on device` (survey of X1-AF66 after jump failed, three times), `state snapshot save failed ... base/16385/16804`, and `market transactions not saved ... base/16385/180600`. Writes succeeded again afterwards (ledger rows 15:22, role persists 15:23, keeper buys 15:26), so it was brief, but it means the database volume is at its limit. Lost: one state snapshot, one batch of market transactions, one system survey (retried). The log buffer only holds 240 min so earlier occurrences are not ruled out. Needs a look at the box's disk (Postgres data volume): free space, table sizes (ledger, market_snapshots, market_transactions, action_events), and old week data.

## 2026-11-14 ~19:10Z: operator decisions for the reset and live changes (operator requests)

- **Applied now:** `resetWindDown` 120 -> 30 min (buying stops 12:30 UTC 11-15); `autoApproveFloorSaleMaxLoss` 600,000 -> 200,000; saved opening template "Opening: auto at reset" (id 6): gate price ceilings 2,000/4,000 -> 3,000/6,000 in both `o3-gatemission` and `o3-gatehaul`; the template call replaced the template by name (no duplicate; first attempt returned Bad Gateway and was confirmed not applied by a read before retrying). Gate buying trigger and cash floors left as they were.
- **Decided for later:** suspend the 3M hull gate and 5M keeper line for 24 h after the gate opens (next week); keep contracts until about hour 96; pilot scrapping 2 ships at T-3 h (10:00 UTC 11-15); hull cutoffs bulk 03:00, hauler 05:00, probes/keepers 08:00 UTC (held by hand; reminders scheduled).
- **Four unbought keeper markets (AH67-DD6B, TQ76-CF7C, QS20-A11F, FT31-A16A):** found a strong lead. `advanceKeeperMarketQueue` drops every market in `coveredKeeperMarkets()` (live keeper pins plus the in-memory `parkedKeeperMarkets` map) before trying to buy, so a market the engine wrongly counts as covered is skipped without any note (my new `mcovered` note sits after that filter). ops keeper_review `next` is empty while ops keepers lists the four as pending with no ship, which fits a stale entry in that map. Not proven; a fix (clear entries whose parked ship is gone) would be a code change.
- **Parking rule (`idleSystems.ts`, report only):** with `freshIdleMinHourlyProfit` 20,000 and a 45 min re-check, a live system with no hour above 20k profit is parked, reopened after 3 h, and parked again if it earns under 15k in 45 min. Keeper rotation is 37-39 min, so a reopened system's prices only refresh near the end of the re-check and it rarely passes. Parking cost the wallet nothing visible (profit held at about 10M/h while keepers fell by 104), and the API budget is not binding (polling 122 of 150). A longer re-check (60-75 min) or relaxing the rule near the end is the lever for next week; nothing changed.

The top routes right now are in freshly released VD64 (FOOD 345k, FABRICS 317k, CLOTHING 233k a trip); VD64 sells already earn about 143k per sale. ANTIMATTER routes from DR91 and F48 are held behind the gate. So more fresh systems is the lever, as you said.
- **Finding 4, the bottleneck is tour ships:** staged systems cannot be released until priced (60%), and the tour dispatcher logged "4 of 4 ships already working" / "no free ship within 12 hops" for DR11, DR91, F48. Releases did happen (CM28 22:11, DA78 22:41), so the pipe works but is paced by tours.

Raised `freshTours` 4 -> 8 (clause max; takes effect within 5 minutes; read back from the set reply). Ships are reused by the engine: it moves a ship on once a system is 60% priced and it has stayed 20 min. 3 of the 5 new explorers were still docked at their yards at 23:03; some light shuttles run at 39-80/300 fuel, and ones under 25% fuel are skipped as "low on fuel". ## 2026-11-15 05:00Z: hauler cutoff (T-8h) and the slow keeper reads

- The scheduled cutoff fired: no light hauler or other trading-hull buys from now. Only probe and keeper buys remain until 08:00. `get_approvals` at 05:01: none open. Explorers bought for tour duty at 02:25 are the last hull buys.
- **Slow keeper reads (operator: "figure out right away"):** keepers ask about 103 calls a minute and complete about 12 reads a minute (26 at 20:12, 17 at 03:12), so a rotation takes 75-80 min against the top-up limit of 60 and top-up added nothing after 03:42. `ops_profile` at about 05:00: uptime 185 min (restart about 01:55), 11,167 CPU-seconds since boot, about one core flat out; heap 882 MB, RSS 2.7 GB of a 4.1 GB limit; event-loop delay p99 0.5 s. The core tick takes about 35 s (max 42 s): `dispatcher.recompute` 19.7 s (documented as synchronous, fleet.ts:11197) plus `computeDispatchRoutes` 14.8 s (recent average 12 s); `maybeTopUpKeepers` took 7.1 s in a maintenance pass. Stored route legs grew from about 577k to 647k as live systems went from 150 to 162. Likely cause: the single-process CPU limit, growing with every released system. Not proven (the low event-loop delay argues against a fully blocked loop). No doctrine clause found that reduces the route computation; a fix is code (cache or coarsen the recompute, or move it off the main thread) and needs a deploy. Operator asked whether to push to main despite the freeze or wait for the reset: unanswered. Nothing changed.

### INCIDENT 2026-11-16 22:38 UTC onward: trader stall, trading time lost (hour 33.6 onward; still open at 00:25 UTC, hour 35.4)
**Read this next week: this week lost at least 1 h 47 min of trading, and it was still going when this was written. Update the end time below when it is fixed.**
- **Start:** last sale 22:38:51 UTC (THEO-1, COPPER at X1-KJ19-F50); last purchases ~22:50 (final circuitry for the gate). Traders were idle from about 22:50. Hour 22:00 made 42k (22 sales), hours 23:00 and 00:00 made 0.
- **What stopped:** the five traders (THEO-1, THEO-3, THEO-7, THEO-17, THEO-A). They sat in orbit with the same cargo and position from 23:12 on (THEO-1 20 MACHINERY at D44, THEO-7 20 ASSAULT_RIFLES at J59, THEO-3 7 SHIP_PLATING at H53, THEO-17 40 circuitry at I56, THEO-A empty at D44). The dispatcher kept recomputing every minute and kept assigning the same routes, but nothing executed. About 440k of cargo was parked.
- **What still worked:** manual orders (jump_ship, dispatch_ship, buy_ship, refuel), tours' market reads, and keeper-probe delivery by the engine (THEO-18 reached AC26 at 00:09:44, THEO-19 reached TV64 at 00:10:46). The stall hit the traders' own route tasks, and earlier the tour and keeper-delivery holds (23:15 to 23:50), which never hopped on their own.
- **Not the cause (checked):** no 429s, one instance, nothing stuck, no errors in logs, API limiter at about 3-7 calls/min (5-10x lower than when ships work), no approvals open, gate mission complete.
- **Signature:** server uptime 32 h (1,933 min at 00:12). Same pattern as 2026-11-10 01:43 (uptime about 31 h, fixed by a server restart). Suspect: a long-lived process degrading; cause not found. Candidate checks next time: event-loop lag, a stuck await in the per-ship task scheduler, a leaked timer after about 30 h.
- **Cost so far:** trading rate before the stall was about 135-145k/h (hours 19:00-21:00 UTC), so roughly 250k lost by 00:25 and counting; also the post-gate ramp (the point of opening 13 h early) was delayed because the ring tours did not start pricing markets.
- **Detection lag:** the hourly update at 23:12 had matched sales 0 and I did not flag it until the operator said "the hourly is zero and that usually means something is wrong" (00:08). Lesson: a zero-sales hour with traders assigned is a stall; check trade_tempo silentShips and the ledger's last SELL at once.
- **Resolution:** open. Options put to the operator at 00:10: server restart (fixed it on 11-10) or fleet pause then resume as a lighter first try. Nothing done by Claude. Fill in: time fixed, how, and the final lost minutes.
- **Prevention ideas (to TODO):** (1) a watchdog: if traders hold assignments and nothing sold or bought for 20 min, raise a Tower alert and log a loud line; (2) a scheduled restart in a quiet hour before uptime reaches about 28 h (note a restart wipes Tower's hourly history and pauses traders briefly); (3) find the root cause of the ~30 h degradation.

### 2026-11-17 00:52 UTC: stall RESOLVED by server restart (closes the INCIDENT entry above)
- **Restarted by the operator at about 00:46 UTC** (instance uptime 6 min at 00:51:50). First purchase after the restart 00:48:54 (THEO-1, 40 MICROPROCESSORS at F50, 3,173c); first sale 00:51:17 (THEO-1, MICROPROCESSORS at D44, 3,836-3,815c, realized +6.6k and +19.3k). Within 6 min: 4 matched sales (+36k), 2 contract payouts (98k), THEO-A buying EQUIPMENT, tours and keepers refueling. API now about 20 calls/min.
- **Time lost:** last sale 22:38:51 to first sale 00:51:17 = **2 h 12 min with no sales** (traders idle from about 22:50, so about 2 h of real trading). At 135-145k/h that is roughly 270-290k of profit, plus the delayed ring tours and the post-gate ramp. Matched profit was 0 in the 23:00 and 00:00 hourly buckets.
- **The operator's agent's analysis (the box's own agent, relayed by the operator):** the fleet was not crashed but idle in a self-reinforcing way: dispatch assigns a route only to a free trader with fresh prices; if the engine's model has every trader marked mid-flight there is nothing to assign, no API calls go out, nothing refreshes the model, and the process still looks healthy. Confirmed triggers on 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-10: a fetch failed at the moment a ship's arrival was reconciled (navigateTo schedules the wake-up at the ETA once), so the ship stayed "in transit" in the model for ever. The agent added `reconcileOverdueTransits()` (every 60 s, re-reads IN_TRANSIT ships more than 120 s past arrival); it fired 3 times in the last 24 h, so it repairs that case. Tonight had no fetch errors, no 429s, `ops_stuck` clean and the fleet not paused, so it was a variant that loop does not catch: most likely a ship believed in transit with a future or bogus arrival (never overdue), or a task parked with a bad `resumeAt`. Restart fixes it because boot rebuilds the model from the game (`listAllShips`) and re-derives each agent's step.
- **Gap:** twice now the restart happened before the per-ship step and scheduler state could be captured, so the exact cause is still a guess. Proposed by the agent (not built; operator to decide): a stall-capture watchdog, every few minutes, that when calls/min stays under a threshold for about 10 min dumps `dispatch_explain`, the scheduler queue/ready/next-due, each trader's step and the model-versus-game state of in-transit ships to a file.
- **Operator rule for next time:** before restarting, if there is time, capture `ops_ship_live` for each trader, `dispatch_explain`, `ops_logs` and the limiter line (calls/min) so the cause can be found. Hourly check: matched sales 0 with traders assigned = stall (check `ops_trade_tempo` silentShips).
- Hour 35.7. Nothing else changed by Claude.

### 2026-11-17 02:15 UTC: why the "Approve" button on upcoming rows seems to do nothing (operator: AC cards not raised, button useless)
- Read from the code (`approveKeeperPriorityMarket` -> `maybeRequestKeeperProbeForMarket`, fleet.ts ~7965-8105): the button re-runs the normal card request, which has the same single `buyKeeperProbeForMarket` slot. If another market's card is open (02:00:54 YH41-H54, 56,856c) the call returns quietly after a keeper note ("the single request slot is held by ..."); no card, no error in the UI. So with the slot taken, every upcoming-row button press (AC26, TV64, BB74...) is a silent no-op, and "approve" on a row only ever raises a card that must then be approved again.
- Cash rule that keeps everything on cards: `keeperAutoApproveMinCash` 3,000,000 vs wallet about 1.75M, so `autoApproveKeeperProbes` (60,000c cap) never applies; every keeper is a card, one at a time. The operator's 3M rule is deliberate (hull-spend discipline); lowering it to about 1M was proposed, not applied.
- Observed prices: in-system yard probes 52-57k at YH41/AC26 (the engine picks the cheapest keeper-hull yard of the system with a ship docked; KJ19 probes were 29k plus about 12k of jumps).
- Code fix proposed (not written, not deployed): make the upcoming-row Approve buy directly (the click is the approval, no second card) and bypass the single-slot rule for operator-approved markets; show a toast when a request is not raised and why. A deploy to main restarts the box, so this waits for the operator's call.

### My (Claude's) mistakes this week, so they are not repeated
- Said no heavy freighter was for sale without reading the yard list; there was one at B64-CA2B. Read the yard list first.
- Read a keeper count as a share and said RS9 had all its keepers; it had 1 of 8 (13%).
- Said this week's gate opened at hour 47; it opened at about hour 34 (gate_history readings stop at 33.9). Hour 47 was last week.
- Blamed idle traders on low fuel and offered refuels; the operator's rule since 11-17: do not raise fuel at all.
- Explained keeper delays wrongly (a card waits for a ship visit; only two tour ships) and had to be corrected three times (CLAUDE.md, "Keeper cards").
- Let a zero-sales hourly (23:12, 11-16) go unflagged until the operator noticed at 00:08.
- A journal commit hung on a `cat >>` waiting for input, so the 00:55 entry was written late.

### Case study: buying big hulls on a thin wallet (11-17, operator-initiated, "self-inflicted")
The standing rules were: no hull of any kind under a 3M wallet, and big hulls (heavies first) only once the wallet is over 10M.
Both heavies were bought by hand below that line, about 3 hours apart:

| | THEO-A1 | THEO-A7 |
|---|---|---|
| Bought | 11-17 19:24, heavy freighter, B64-CA2B | 11-17 22:11, heavy freighter, B64-CA2B |
| Price | 2,240,596 | 2,303,936 (the yard price rose after the first buy) |
| Wallet before -> after | about 3.20M -> 956,765 | about 2.93M -> 625,128 |

What followed:
- **The hulls themselves were good buys.** A1 returned about 940k matched in its first hour, and the two heavies were the only
  traders clearly covering their jumps overnight (A1 960k matched in the 6 h to 02:46; A7 258k).
- **The wallet was not.** A heavy's load costs 300-400k, so 625k left could be emptied by the two heavies alone. The wallet fell
  to 95,467 at 01:37 and 125,621 at 03:13; wallet plus holds went from about 1.19M (01:13) to 843-863k (02:45-03:13).
- **It landed at the worst time.** The best local routes had decayed (top trips 43-54k at 19:00, 17-25k by 23:00), new systems
  opened far apart, and jump fees rose above trading profit. With no cash cushion there was no room to absorb that: no tour
  ships (about 105-112k each) for the four staged systems still waiting, no keeper cards beyond the cheapest, and traders limited
  by spendable cash.
- **Lesson:** after any big-hull purchase keep a working-capital floor of at least (number of big hulls x one full load, about
  400k) plus about 100k per other trader, so about 2M with today's fleet, on top of the 3M purchase rule. A second big hull waits
  until the first has paid back its price in matched profit and the wallet is back over the line, not three hours later.
  Manual buys bypass every doctrine threshold, so this has to be held by hand (CLAUDE.md, "Cash discipline").

### 2026-11-18 ~03:55 UTC: Finding: the overnight jump losses were route churn, not bad estimates
- Operator asked for the check (expected vs actual per trip). Ops analysis, last 5 h: 111 trips, all with estimates; calibration
  factor 0.84 (trips realized 84% of the profit they were chosen on), so the trade estimates were roughly right. My earlier guess
  that "the estimates did not hold" was wrong too. But positioning (jumps and fuel before the buy) was 908,539 against 441,986 net.
- THEO-1, from ship_movements: after its last sale (~00:42 at PN45) it made 32 gate jumps (191,505c) before its next buy at 02:33
  (only 25 of 40 ANTIMATTER, wallet 140k -> 103k), turning back on itself again and again: SN86 -> BB74 -> SN86 (01:03-01:12),
  AC26 -> GV7 -> AC26 (01:31-01:37), AC26 -> KJ19 -> AC26 -> KJ19 (02:27-02:36), YH41 visited 5 times. That trip was expected to
  make 9,737, made 22,285, and carried 148,407 of positioning. Others the same shape: THEO-38 GOLD expected 481c, positioning 38,501;
  THEO-6C FOOD expected 3,330, positioning 58,484; THEO-6B CLOTHING expected 23,774, positioning 52,610.
- So each single pick was charged for its own jumps (the dispatcher does that), but the ship's target kept changing while it was
  empty, and the jumps already flown toward the old target were wasted. The commitment that should hold an empty ship to its
  pick lapses when the trader itself refuses the route (trader.ts findRoute -> whyNotViable -> declineRoute -> dispatcher.decline
  deletes the commitment and hands it something else next cycle).
- Why it refused is not proven: the in-memory log buffer no longer holds 00:40-02:40. Leading suspect: cash. The dispatcher prices a
  route on the ship's full hold; with the wallet at 95-270k between 01:37 and 02:33 the trader could afford only part of a load
  (e.g. 40 ANTIMATTER at ~5k = 200k), so whyNotViable's "profit <= 0 at N units" or "volume=0" check refused it and the ship was
  re-routed. Second suspect: "skipping buy: price now above snapshot" (dead route) at far markets. Either way the thin wallet and
  the churn are connected.
- Not changed: nothing. Next, operator to decide: (1) persist trader route refusals (reason, ship, route) so the next one can be
  proven, (2) then fix whichever cause it is (size dispatch picks against spendable cash, or keep an empty ship committed while it
  is mid-positioning).

- CORRECTION (operator: "Look at the screenshot, the wallet isn't 84,000. It's 400,000."): the operator was right and I was wrong. The ledger shows the wallet at 103,199 after 04:40:45, about 84k for ~20 s after three jumps at 04:42:04-08, then 411,572 after THEO-38's sale at 04:42:28-31. My 84,032 (get_cargo_value) was read after that sale: it was FleetManager.walletCredits(), the engine's cached balance, which only refreshes on a 30 s agent read. The 04:43 update's "wallet 84k" was wrong for the same reason. Fix (feature branch, needs main): every trade response's balance now also updates the engine's wallet (FleetManager.noteCredits via api.onCredits), and the cash warning names the cargo value. Lesson: check a surprising figure against the ledger's wallet_after before reporting it.

- 2026-11-18 11:45 UTC: operator: "I made the change last night. And it should've been fine." (positioningHops 3 -> 8 -> 6 at 05:17). Right in principle: the dispatcher nets each ship's positioning jumps. Traced why it still lost money, from the logs (buffer covers 10:20-11:37) and the code:
  - THEO-38, 10:22-10:53: sold ANTIMATTER at XV14 (+21.7k), then the dispatcher gave it nothing every minute ("idle ... got nothing", the far routes are canJump=false / not worth it for this ship). The trader then claimed its own route each time and flew one hop toward it: ANTIMATTER UF11 (hop 1/6), FUEL ZA16 (1/6), ALUMINUM_ORE QM40 (1/4), IRON QM40 (1/3, 1/2), LAB_INSTRUMENTS HS78 (1/5), ANTIMATTER UF11 again (1/6, 1/5). Nine jumps, no buy.
  - Cause in code: trader.findRoute() falls back to dispatcher.claim() when it has no assignment. claim() takes the first route in the fleet-wide ranked list that the trader's viableRoute() accepts. viableRoute() checks reachability within positioningHops and prices only the buy -> sell leg (routeProfit = gross - slippage - tripCost(buyAt, sellAt)); the jumps from where the ship stands to the buy market are not subtracted. And claim() sets no commitment, so the next minute's recompute (which sees the ship idle and uncommitted) drops it, and after the hop the trader claims whatever is first from its new spot. With positioningHops 3 the claimable set was small and near; at 6 it reaches far routes, and the claim changes after almost every hop.
  - Second, smaller path: discoverPrices() (when nothing is claimable) jumps to the first reachable market in another system with no cost check; THEO-1 ping-ponged MV84 <-> XD69 this way (8 of the 10 discovery jumps 10:20-11:37). 10 of 67 trader jumps in that window were discovery jumps; most of the rest were the claim zigzag above.
  - So the setting was not wrong by design; it exposed a trader-side path that ignores positioning cost. Proposed: stopgap positioningHops back to 3; fix: claim() and viableRoute() net here -> buyAt jumps, a claim gets the same commitment as a dispatcher assignment, discovery stays in-system unless a jump pays.

- 2026-11-18 ~12:20 UTC: operator asked for a code analysis: bad luck, bad decisions, or code. Findings (checked, with what could not be checked):
  - Checked and dropped: "low cash made traders fly short loads". Purchases 04:19-11:08 grouped by trip: holds were 84-107% full every hour until 10:00.
  - Code: the dispatcher never reads the wallet when scoring (only a circuit's second leg checks cash); the trader sizes by what it can afford and declines a route that no longer pays at that size (15-minute decline), and with no assignment falls back to self-claim (no positioning cost, no commitment) and cross-system discovery. Those fallbacks fire only when the dispatcher has nothing for a trader; at 11:24-12:04 the recompute showed 10 of 12 traders "waiting" with no assignment. In 10:20-11:42, 43 of 67 trader jumps came straight from a self-claim (33) or discovery (10). Earlier hours cannot be split this way (log buffer rolled), so the share before 10:20 is unknown.
  - Last week ran positioningHops 10 all week without this; the same fallbacks existed. Likely difference (not proven): last week the dispatcher had work for every trader (rich fresh routes, millions in cash), so the fallbacks rarely fired; this week thin routes, route resting (2 trips/route/hour, 3 buys/market/hour) and a small wallet left traders unassigned, and the fallbacks did the jumping.
  - Decisions: the two heavy-freighter buys (11-17 19:24 and 22:11) left about 0.6-1M working capital; the heavies themselves were the top earners. The 05:17 positioningHops 3 -> 6 change widened the fallbacks' reach; by itself it would have been safe.

- 2026-11-18 12:15 UTC: CORRECTION to the ~12:20 analysis entry: a declined route does not leave the trader unassigned for 15 minutes. DECLINE_MS (15 min) blocks only that leg (or the whole good when the refusal was a protected good) for that ship; the next recompute (about a minute) can hand it anything else (isDeclinedBy in dispatcher.ts). The idle traders had no other route worth giving after jumps were netted (at 11:44 the only work listed was THEO-37 JEWELRY and THEO-3 contract FOOD).

- 2026-11-18 ~15:35 UTC: operator: "why are all the traders unassigned; something we changed this week?" Checked: no engine commit since the 11-15 reset touches route selection (the dispatcher is unchanged; 4ff600b only keeps the engine's wallet copy current). Cross-system: every route was refused "the first trip does not cover the N-jump positioning cost" (THEO-A7 in QM40: MEDICINE FD13 -> AH21 31.9k against 4 jumps ~32k). The dispatcher sizes a route's profit to the cash budget (fleet.ts priceLeg, min(hold, budget/buyPrice, volume x lots)), so a thin wallet shrinks every first trip below its positioning cost; both rules predate 10-29 and never bit with millions in the wallet. Local: live keeper prices (15:06-15:34) show no in-system spread: BB74 IRON buy 157 / best sell 160, ELECTRONICS 2,906 / 2,947, MACHINERY 3,165 / 2,901, FABRICS 2,723 / 2,609, PLASTICS 212 / 213, ALUMINUM 278 / 254; TV64 ELECTRONICS 4,443 / 2,795, FABRICS 3,743 / 2,325; HS78 MACHINERY 6,020 / 3,214; supply SCARCE almost everywhere. Rivals traded ~375k units in 24 h across our markets (SPECTER 78 ships, VEPRINE 33, HYDRA in TV64, NVIATE in SN86, ASTROBJORN/MARAUDER in YH41). So the engine reads the markets right: the local spreads are gone, and the far routes fail the cash-sized positioning test.

### 2026-11-18 18:40 UTC: Recovery log, the moves in order (input for a self-healing recovery module)

Operator request (18:40): record every recovery move in detail so the engine can later learn to heal itself. Each move below
gives the **trigger** (what we saw), the **move**, and the **outcome** with numbers. The "Rules a module could encode" list
at the end is the draft spec. Detailed entries for each time are above; this is the summary across all of them.

**The state we were recovering from.** On 11-18 at 04:12, wallet plus cargo was 1,603,301. By 15:12 the wallet was 6,707.
From 07:00 to 11:00 there were six losing hours in a row, each about -180k to -290k: per hour, 54-84 sales making 158-258k
against 58-68 jumps costing 359-426k. The cause was positioning jumps (route churn), not bad trade estimates. Calibration was
0.84, and positioning cost 908,539 in 5 h against a net of 441,986. The old systems had no local spreads left, because
rivals (SPECTER, VEPRINE, HYDRA) had flattened them.

| Time (UTC) | Trigger | Move | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| 11:41 | Jump fees larger than trade profit | positioningHops 6 -> 3 | Jumps fell, but so did sales: 12:13 had 0 sales |
| 12:09 | Parked probes holding capital | Scrapped THEO-27 and THEO-23; traders set local-only | 12:29: THEO-37 sold 80 JEWELRY for +43.7k; 13:12 was the first positive hour (+22.1k) |
| 13:20 | Big hulls idle under local-only | Reach any for THEO-A1, -A7, -6A, -6B, -6C, -7, -A, -17, -38 | 14:12 -44.4k (fuel 49.3k on 173 refuels, 9 sales) |
| 14:20-14:45 | Traders re-pricing their systems for nothing | positioningHops 6; traderIdleDiscovery built and set off; tradeBudget | 15:12: 0 sales, 5 jumps 39.9k, wallet 6.7k |
| 15:47 | Nine traders idle, no spreads | Wave 1: four idle traders toured new systems (THEO-7 to JD68, THEO-1 to CN92, THEO-17 to QA67, THEO-6C to BQ46) | Tour jumps 51.1k in the 16:13 hour; THEO-17 bug found (#46) |
| 16:25 | THEO-6C's BQ46 had been priced | #46 fix deployed (858817f); BQ46 released | 6C made 2 small sales in BQ46 (5.8k) |
| 17:40 | Wallet 60.9k; 10 jumps in the hour although "traders should not jump without a route" | (diagnosis) a priced route plus 1-2 positioning jumps still passed the dispatcher's first-trip test | Led to the jump cash guard |
| 17:47 | JD68, CN92 and QA67 priced | Released all three | Small routes appeared: GOLD JD68->KY80 12.3k, FERTILIZERS BQ46->QM40 6.5k, PLASTICS QA67->VM8 4k |
| ~18:00 | Wallet 21.5k at 17:56 | Every trader local-only; jump cash guard built; 8 parked keepers scrapped | Scrap income about 65-80k; jumps stopped (none from ~17:55 to 18:20) |
| 18:10 | Guard ready | 57d3d03 to main; jumpCashFloor 100,000 ON | No new jump routes under 100k |
| 18:20 | Wallet 79.9k; contract would need about 272-350k | FOOD contract held until ~750k (THEO-3 stays pinned, in YH41); THEO-A1 pinned by hand to MEDICINE FD13-XB2B -> AH21-A1 | See the A1 trips below |
| 18:26-18:32 | A1's first trip | Bought 18 MEDICINE at 3,420 (61,560), jumped (6,275), sold 18 at 4,999 (89,982) | **+28,422 trade profit**; wallet 147,696 at 18:32 |
| 18:37 | A1 empty at AH21-A1 while FOOD (1,620, ABUNDANT) is there for FD13-XB2B (2,162) | Too late: the pinned MEDICINE route sent it home empty (jump 5,476 at 18:37:42) | Lost a paid return leg worth ~11.6k; operator approved switching to FOOD after the next sale |

**THEO-A1 detail.** It bought 18, not 225: the most likely cause is `cashFloorTradeSharePct` 50%. Half of the ~128.7k wallet
is ~64k, and 64k at 3,420 is 18.8 units. At 147.7k the next buy was sized at 21 units (log 18:37:36). The MEDICINE source is
cheap because SPECTER sold 300 units into FD13-XB2B at 14:30, pushing it from 5,251 down to 746. The trip's profit grows with
the wallet. The dispatcher now values AH21-A1 at 22,981 a trip, up from 19,444 at 18:18.

**What did not work.** Reach any on the big hulls with no spreads near them: they flew to thin routes and paid jumps. The
positioningHops changes on their own: the first-trip test compares a cash-sized trip with the jumps, and when cash is low the
trip is small. Tours while cash was low: a wave-1 jump cost about 6-7k per hop, and the systems gave only small routes.

**What worked.** Stopping jumps (local-only, then the jump cash guard). Turning idle capital into cash (scrapping parked
keepers and probes). Hand-picking one route for a big hull, priced for both legs, inside a two-system pair one gate apart.
Holding the contract until cash can fund it.

**Rules a module could encode (draft, nothing built).**
1. Detect: net negative for 2 hours (matched profit minus jumps and fuel), or wallet under X% of its 24 h peak. Enter recovery mode.
2. In recovery mode, stop positioning jumps: traders local-only, and a jump cash floor scaled to the size of a trip.
3. Free capital: scrap or sell idle hulls (parked keepers, probes) down to a minimum.
4. Concentrate cash: pick the 1-2 best **loops** (A->B and B->A both pay, one gate apart), not one-way routes, and pin the
   biggest hull(s) already near them. Score a loop by (both legs' profit) minus 2 jumps, sized by the cash-share rule.
5. Re-pin at each end of the loop (the return leg) and don't fly home empty. Today this is done by hand, and it was missed once (18:37).
6. Hold long-capital jobs (contracts, missions) until the wallet passes a threshold (operator: ~750k for the FOOD contract).
7. Leave recovery mode when the wallet passes a threshold, then restore reach and normal positioning one step at a time.

This time the empty return was caught: at 18:50 the pin was switched to FOOD X1-AH21-A1 -> X1-FD13-XB2B (dispatcher 17,393/trip) while the ship was still docked at A1 (operator approval at 18:40). The stale ones are mostly 1-4 good fuel/yard stops; JV28 is the exception (3 of 5 markets never priced). **The local spreads are inverted**: in each system, the cheapest place to buy a good costs more than the best place to sell it. TV64, 12 goods checked, all inverted, e.g. FOOD buy 2,676 (K88, SCARCE) vs sell 2,131 (J61); ELECTRONICS 4,729 vs 2,795; MEDICINE 6,072 vs 3,932; IRON 232 vs 146; CLOTHING 6,163 vs 4,782. The one positive spread was BB74 ELECTRONICS, 2,906 to 2,947 (+41/unit, gone after one lot of slippage). The exporters are SCARCE: rivals and our own week have drained them, and import markets still pay less than the exports cost. So this is not a pricing bug. Recovery needs time for the exports to refill, or fresh systems. For the module: "no local route" plus "prices fresh" plus "exports SCARCE" means drained markets; don't burn jumps chasing them.

- 2026-11-18 19:18 UTC (recovery log, THEO-7 GOLD, my mistake): THEO-7 bought 80 GOLD at JD68-C36Z at 19:15 (238 each, 19,040) and is flying to the JD68 gate (19:29), hop 1 of **3** to KY80 (JD68 -> DQ31 -> PN45 -> KY80). With jumps at ~6.5k for this hull (its 16:10 jump cost 6,490), 3 jumps (~19.5k) eat the whole spread: 80 x (374 - 238) = 10.9k gross, so about **-8.6k** on the leg. Cause: the dispatcher's profitPerTrip is sized to the biggest trader hull (fleet.ts priceLeg: units = min(maxTraderCargo, affordable, volume x lots)), i.e. 225 units on a heavy. For an 80-unit ship a 3-jump route in the work list can be a loser. I read the list figure as THEO-7's and did not check the hop count. Dumping the gold in JD68 (bid 130) returns the same cash as going on (~10.4k back), so the ship carries on. The FERTILIZERS return is NOT worth pinning: an empty jump KY80 -> PN45 (~6.5k) against ~5.7k. Lesson for the module: score a pinned route at the ship's own hold and its own hop count.

- **02:10 FOUND: the dispatcher's positioning check mixes units and refuses rich far routes.** ANTIMATTER AB12-Z25E -> XS51-F23F is
  listed at 160,578/trip; THEO-A7 (225, in XS51, 5 jumps from AB12) is refused "net score at or below zero after positioning".
  In `scoreItem` (src/engine/dispatcher.ts ~1908-1930) the score is the route's time-scaled score (scoreRoute: profit x 600 s /
  tripSeconds = 29,737 here), discounted again for the positioning time, and then the positioning JUMP CREDITS (5 jumps ~33k) are
  subtracted in full, un-scaled. A per-600-second rate minus a lump of credits: 29.7k x (trip/(trip+positioning)) - 33k < 0, so a
  trip that really nets ~127k after positioning is refused. Same code since at least 2026-10-29 (shallow-clone boundary), so not
  new this week; it bites now because this week's rich systems are 4-6 jumps apart. Fix: subtract positioning credits on the same
  scale (score = (profit - positioningCredits) x 600/(trip + positioning)), behind a doctrine clause, off by default, tested.

- **03:40 THEO-7 made a local trader in XS51** (arrived; the 02:24 check never ran because this session's worker restarted
  several times 02:12-03:40). **Fix built for the positioning units bug** (feature branch, not deployed): clause
  `positioningCostScaled`, off by default; also fixes the same mix-up in "the first trip does not cover the N-jump positioning
  cost" (#44's root). With it on, THEO-A7 is assigned the 160k ANTIMATTER route in the replayed test. Needs the operator's go to
  deploy (restart), then the clause turned on.
